- `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/gpu_model_runner.py`. This conflates two real files that
  this tree also cites separately, `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/model_runner.py` and
  `vllm/v1/worker/gpu_model_runner.py`.

Group C is the only part of the first pass's C5 that survives as a defect, and it
is three anchors rather than twelve.

## 9. Gateability of the target

**`gateable = no`.** The shallow-checkout blocker this section originally
described IS gone. **A different and harder one was found in its place when the
target was actually built: its runtime requirements do not install on aarch64,
and every fleet device is aarch64.** See §9.2. The text below is the step-4
reading and is preserved as written; §9.1 and §9.2 are the measurement.

AGENTS.md: an oracle is gateable only once it demonstrably builds and runs the
model, and constructing a config proves nothing. Nobody has built
`cdefd9d499`, or `adebc41b7e` before it. The evidence remains that the tree
contains no reference to either SHA outside this report and its predecessor.

What the unshallow changed, and what it did not:

- **Changed.** A shallow clone cannot produce the `vllm_runtime_version` string
  the `parity-pin` block requires. `setuptools_scm` cannot count commits since
  the last tag and falls back to a default, which is exactly the open
  discrepancy in [#1185](https://github.com/mudler/vllm.cpp/issues/1185): one
  measured build inside an `rc` lease reported `0.1.dev1+g555967922` against the
  recorded `0.23.1rc1.dev1511+g555967922`. With the full history the tag count is
  available, so a build from this checkout can now report the real string. The
  `+g<sha>` constraint `assert_oracle_commit` enforces is satisfiable for the
  target.
- **Not changed.** No build has run. No `vllm.__version__` has been measured at
  the target, no wheel exists, no venv, and no run on any fleet device. A
  reachable history is a precondition for the version string, not a substitute
  for the build.

Two dependency moves under the denominator, measured from the manifests at both
revisions:

| Dependency | at pin | at target |
|---|---|---|
| `flashinfer-python`, `flashinfer-cubin` | `0.6.15.post1` | `0.6.18` |
| `transformers` floor | `>= 5.5.3` | `>= 5.10.4` |
| `torch` | `2.13.0` | `2.13.0`, unchanged |

The `parity-pin` block records `flashinfer_version = 0.6.15.post1`, so advancing
the pin moves FlashInfer under every benchmark baselined against it. Step 6 of
the cycle requires the vLLM baseline to be re-measured at the target before any
comparison, and this is the concrete reason. `6a962071bd` vllm#52998 additionally
flips `VLLM_ALLREDUCE_USE_FLASHINFER` from 0 to 1, which moves the vLLM
production configuration that serves as the denominator for any multi-GPU parity
run.

The measurement is owed by
[#2490](https://github.com/mudler/vllm.cpp/issues/2490) and is a step-6
obligation, not a step-4 one. The pin's own oracle file keeps `gateable = yes` at
`555967922`, which remains true and is not edited here.

### 9.1 First build attempt, 2026-09-01 (PINPORT, #2524)

**`gateable` is still `no`.** The first build of `cdefd9d499` that anyone has
attempted ran inside an `rc` lease on `orin:gpu0`. It had not finished when this
was written, so no `vllm.__version__` exists yet and nothing here claims one.
What it DID establish is recorded because it is measured, and because the next
attempt should not re-derive it:

```console
HEAD_SHA=cdefd9d4997f00da72dc6245cc60678b50761b7e
SHALLOW=false
REVCOUNT=20692
GIT_DESCRIBE=v0.28.1rc0-233-gcdefd9d49
CLONE_RC=0   CO_RC=0   VENV_RC=0   PIPUP_RC=0
SMI_RC=127
```

- **The clone is genuinely at the target and genuinely complete.** `REVCOUNT`
  reproduces §1's `git rev-list --count cdefd9d499` = 20692 exactly, from an
  independent clone on a different machine. `SHALLOW=false` means
  `setuptools_scm` can count commits since the last tag, so the `+g<sha>` string
  the `parity-pin` block requires is obtainable and #1185's fallback-version
  defect does not apply to this build.
- **The target is vLLM 0.28.x, not the 0.26.x this file's prose describes.**
  `git describe` puts the target 233 commits after `v0.28.1rc0`. The active pin's
  prose says "vLLM 0.26.0.dev0" while its measured runtime string is
  `0.23.1rc1.dev1511+g555967922`, so release prose and measured string already
  disagree by design here. Two release lines moved across this range, which is
  worth knowing before anyone reasons about API stability over it.
- **`GIT_DESCRIBE` is NOT the version string and must not be transcribed into
  the `parity-pin` block.** The block is written from a source build's measured
  `vllm.__version__`, never from a release number or a tag distance. This line
  exists to say what the build is expected to report, so a mismatch is visible
  when the real measurement arrives.
- **`nvidia-smi` is absent from the leased container on `orin:gpu0`**
  (`SMI_RC=127`). That is a property of that worker image, not of the target. It
  matters because "demonstrably builds and runs the model" needs the run half,
  so the run leg was queued on `thor:gpu0` rather than kept on `orin`.

The dependency install is the long pole: the job reached `BUILDDEPS` at t=422s
and the moved FlashInfer (`0.6.18`) and `transformers` floor (`5.10.4`) are
installed there. Whoever picks this up should read the job back with
`rc logs <FULL job id>` — a prefix returns nothing, silently.

**Second reading, t=3128s.** The dependency install SUCCEEDED and the job moved
on to the runtime requirements:

```console
BUILDDEPS_RC=0
TORCH_VER=2.13.0+cu130   TORCHQ_RC=0
CUDA_AVAIL=False   DEV=none   CAP=none
```

- **Torch resolves to `2.13.0+cu130` at the target**, which confirms from a real
  install what §9's table read from the manifests: torch does NOT move across
  this range. The two dependencies that DO move, FlashInfer to `0.6.18` and the
  `transformers` floor to `5.10.4`, installed without error.
- **The leased container on `orin:gpu0` has no GPU visible to torch.**
  `CUDA_AVAIL=False` with `DEV=none` agrees with `SMI_RC=127`, so this is the
  worker image and not a transient. **That settles where gateability can be
  measured: `orin` can answer the BUILD half and can never answer the RUN half.**
  A build there yields a `vllm.__version__` and nothing else; "demonstrably
  builds and runs the model" needs a device the container can see.
- Consequence for planning: the run leg must land on a worker whose container
  exposes the GPU, and that must be confirmed with `torch.cuda.is_available()`
  BEFORE the queue wait, not after. A job that spends an hour installing
  dependencies and only then discovers `CUDA_AVAIL=False` has burned the lease
  for a version string.

### 9.2 The job finished, and the target does NOT install on this fleet

Final result after 5248s (~87 min):

```console
BUILDDEPS_RC=0   RUNDEPS_RC=1   BUILD_RC=0   IMPORT_RC=1   EXT_RC=1
```

**`BUILD_RC=0` is not a pass, and reading it as one would be the exact trap this
repository has recorded before.** The `pip install -e .` step returned 0, but
`import vllm` returned 1 and `import vllm._C` returned 1. A build that cannot
import is not an oracle. **No `vllm.__version__` was obtained. `gateable`
remains `no`.**

**The blocker is a new RUNTIME dependency that does not exist for aarch64.**
`RUNDEPS_RC=1` is `instanttensor`, which ships no aarch64 wheel and fails to
build from source:

```text
error: Command '['aarch64-linux-gnu-g++', ... '-c', 'csrc/loader_common.cpp', ...]'
       returned non-zero exit status 1.
ERROR: Failed building wheel for instanttensor
```

**This is a change inside our range, and it is the kind a sync cycle exists to
catch.** Measured at both revisions:

| Revision | Where `instanttensor` appears | Platform guard |
|---|---|---|
| pin `5559679229` | `requirements/test/*` only | `test/cuda.in`: `platform_machine == "x86_64"` |
| target `cdefd9d499` | **`requirements/cuda.txt`**, the runtime deps | **none** |

`2d7f42b4f3` vllm#52801 `[Build] Add InstantTensor to CUDA dependencies` promoted
it from a test dependency to a runtime one. The test-requirements entry that
declares it x86_64-only was left in place, so upstream still marks the package
x86-only in one file while requiring it unconditionally in another.

**Consequence, and it is larger than one job.** Every device on this fleet is
aarch64: `dgx:gpu0` is a GB10 Grace-Blackwell, `thor:gpu0` and `orin:gpu0` are
Jetson. So the target's runtime requirements do not install on ANY fleet device
as things stand, and the queued build-and-run leg on `thor` will fail the same
way for the same reason. **Advancing the pin to `cdefd9d499` would move the
oracle to a revision this fleet cannot install**, which is a stronger reason to
hold the pin than the unported queue is.

Three ways out, none of them free, for whoever takes this next:

1. Install the runtime requirements with `instanttensor` excluded and confirm
   nothing on the paths we gate imports it. It is a fast safetensors loader, so
   the honest check is whether model loading still works, not whether `import
   vllm` succeeds.
2. Build `instanttensor` for aarch64 and find out whether its source failure is
   the package or the toolchain in that container image. The compile that failed
   is `csrc/loader_common.cpp` under `aarch64-linux-gnu-g++`.
3. Measure the oracle on x86_64 instead. That changes the denominator's machine
   and therefore every benchmark baselined on it, so it is a decision rather than
   a workaround.

Report it upstream as well: requiring a package unconditionally while the
project's own test requirements mark it `x86_64` looks like an oversight in
vllm#52801, not a deliberate platform drop.

**CORRECTED 2026-09-02 by the PINWINDOW wave
([#2589](https://github.com/mudler/vllm.cpp/issues/2589)), from a measurement,
and the correction matters because it changes which of the three ways out is
cheap.** The sentence above says `instanttensor` "ships no aarch64 wheel and
fails to build from source". Half of that is right and half of it is not. There
is no aarch64 wheel. It DOES build from source on aarch64: the compile above
fails on `fatal error: Python.h: No such file or directory`, which is the leased
worker image lacking `python3-dev`, not the package lacking the platform. That
answers way out 2's question -- "the package or the toolchain in that container
image" -- with "the toolchain", and it costs one `apt-get install`:

```console
ITENSOR_NODEV_RC=1     # Python.h absent, the failure recorded above
APTDEV_RC=0            # apt-get install -y python3-dev
PYTHON_H_AFTER=present
ITENSOR_DEV_RC=0       # Successfully installed instanttensor-0.1.9
```

Measured in an `rc` lease on `orin:gpu0`, job
`080e1cd7-0fab-483e-a74f-507d356996a5`, as a trailing non-gating probe placed
after every gating leg of that job so it could not contaminate them. It is a
probe of the PACKAGE and the worker image, not of a vLLM revision, which is why
a job at a different target can answer it.

**Say what this does and does not establish.** It establishes that
`instanttensor 0.1.9` compiles and installs on aarch64 once `python3-dev` is
present. It does NOT establish that `cdefd9d499`'s whole `requirements/cuda.txt`
then installs; nobody has re-run `RUNDEPS` at the head with the header package
in place, and until someone does, `RUNDEPS_RC=1` at the head is a measurement and
its repair is a hypothesis. `2d7f42b4f3` vllm#52801 is still an unguarded runtime
dependency with an `x86_64` marker in the project's own test requirements, so it
is still worth reporting upstream. The unworked PORT-NOW queue remains a reason
to hold the pin whatever the answer. See
[`2026-09-02-db92053.md`](2026-09-02-db92053.md) §7.

## 10. Carry-over

A cycle that stalls keeps the old pin and records what is left. This one stops at
step 4 by design. Two of the first report's seven carry-over items are resolved
and are named as such; the rest stand.

**RESOLVED. C1, the invisible window.** 963 commits between 2026-07-26 and
2026-08-18 were absent from the checkout. They are enumerated and classified
here. The 212 already-ported files that changed with +19509 and -6591 lines now
have commits behind them.

**RESOLVED. C2, the one UNCLASSIFIED commit.** `41f179b57a` is a normal commit
with a parent. Its real diff touches no mirrored path, so it is correctly outside
the universe. See §1.

**RESOLVED IN PART. C5, twelve unverifiable anchors.** Nine of the twelve are
stale-but-real and each now names the upstream commit that removed it (§8). Three
remain genuinely wrong: `vllm/tests/kernels/attention/test_mla_decode_cpu.py`,
`vllm/multimodal/media.py` and `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/gpu_model_runner.py`. Recorded,
not repaired.

**C3. Steps 5, 6 and 7 are not started** (still true of PORTING as of
2026-09-01; the PINPORT wave did the deviation reconciliation §11 item 1 names,
and nothing else in steps 5 to 7)**.** No PORT-NOW entry is ported, no file
header is bumped, no `parity-ledger.md` row is appended, no golden dump is
regenerated and the pin does not move. This report is the queue for step 5.

**C4. `porting-inventory.md` is not edited.** All 356 INVENTORY entries owe a
`vllm#` reference and a tier, and the four §7 deviations owe their updates. The
block that made this impossible in the first pass is gone, so this is now
schedulable work rather than a dependency. It belongs in the cycle that advances
the pin, because the guide makes inventory annotation part of the cycle and a
tiered inventory is what decides which INVENTORY entries become T0 or T1.

**C6. Tier-A completeness still rests on our own citations.** Carried forward
unchanged and deliberately. The 369 ported paths were derived from `vllm/**.py`
strings inside `src/`, `include/` and `tools/`. A ported C++ file that cites no
upstream path is invisible to that derivation and its upstream file falls into
Tier B. The tree has 916 files under `src/` against 385 distinct cited paths, so
the mapping is not one-to-one and the bound is real. It was not measured. No claim
here depends on it being tight: a missed ported file demotes a commit from
PORT-NOW to INVENTORY, never the reverse, so **315 PORT-NOW is a lower bound**.

**C7. No disposition was independently re-derived.** The three checks in §3 prove
the SHAs, the PR numbers and the reuse are real. None of them proves a disposition
is correct. Twelve readers each flagged their own contested calls, and the
recurring shape is a commit whose ported-file diff is a behavior-neutral API
widening while the behavior lands in an unported file. Those are the mutation
targets for the fresh review. Named examples: `a311916a29` and `827a2af806`
(filed INVENTORY despite large ported-file diffs), `80d6d557f3` (filed IGNORE,
but it changes the `lm_head` module identity for tied models across ten ported
files), `79c865b838` (filed IGNORE as cudagraph plumbing on a ported model file),
and `dec13a33b7` (filed INVENTORY, but carries two incidental fixes to ported
spec-decode files).

**C8, new. Two revert pairs are fully contained in the range and must not be
double-counted.** `47ececb58e` vllm#48223 is reverted by `3d204dfdaa` vllm#52024,
and `bebf918044` vllm#50352 is superseded by `9a4fd57cac` vllm#50688. Both are
filed IGNORE with that reason. `d53b1c2efc` / `e3f6026503` and `ca90b9e7d4` /
`d04d3e0a07` are the same shape. Porting either half of a pair would land a
capability the target does not carry.

**C9, new. One ordering hazard inside the PORT-NOW queue.** `5789897aa4`
vllm#49969 adds a `num_speculative_tokens >= dspark_block_size` rejection;
`68ca6fd02c` vllm#50869 removes it and `51def7848a` vllm#52223 re-lands the
removal. Ported out of order, the queue installs a validation and then deletes it.

**C10, new, added 2026-09-01 by the PINPORT wave (#2524). The queue needs
re-deriving against this tree before it is worked.** C7 says no disposition was
independently re-derived. §4 now carries the measurement: on a stratified sample
of 32 of the 315 PORT-NOW entries, 5 are real code gaps, 4 are already satisfied
and 23 are not applicable. A wave that works the list in the order written will
spend most of its budget confirming non-gaps, and — worse — the three shapes that
produce a non-gap are not visible from the upstream diff alone, so the confirming
is not cheap. Re-derivation is therefore a step of its own, and it belongs before
the porting rather than inside it. The 315 figure remains the correct count of
commits to ADJUDICATE. It is not the count of ports.

**PINPORT's own carry-over.** That wave reconciled the four §7 deviations,
measured C10, and filed #2527 for the one live correctness bug it found. It
ported nothing and did not move the pin. It could not build: the dev box was at
load ~50 with two other worktrees compiling and ~1 GiB free, and a third build is
this repository's recorded OOM failure. The vllm#49206 fix is written and
verified against the upstream diff, but it is UNCOMPILED and was deliberately not
landed; the patch is attached to #2527 and still owes a red test, a build and a
focused green.

## 11. What the next cycle does, in order

1. **Reconcile the four deviations in §7** in the same cycle as the advance, as
   the guide requires. Three of them name this cycle as their own discharge and
   each now has an immutable merge SHA to reconcile onto.
2. **Take the PORT-NOW queue in dependency order, not SHA order.** The KV cache
   layout refactor is a numbered series and the range contains three of its
   stages. Stages 1 to 3 (`4dcd10eb0d` vllm#44454, `51878e5b6e` vllm#44455,
   `6700813f86` vllm#44456) are already at the pin. Take the rest in their own
   order:
   - `61874f9842` vllm#51612, stage 4: promotion through a class-changing
     `replace_as`, which changes the promoted spec's page size for quantized and
     chunked-local groups.
   - `57bd0ed441` vllm#51704, stage 5: page-size math around `num_head_slots` and
     `state_content_bytes`, backends publishing packing via `customize_spec`,
     `TQFullAttentionSpec` deleted.
   - `8bdc70ec7b` vllm#51718, stage 6 and the largest single item in the queue at
     1797 lines over 30 ported files: `storage_block_size` becomes
     `num_states` / `tokens_per_state` / `state_content_size_bytes` plus an
     explicit `KVCacheLayout`.
   - `e126687a9a` vllm#53896 lands on the same surface, reworking
     `kv_cache_utils.py` at +193/-156, and follows the series.
   Then `ef47a897e2` vllm#51875, which makes the prefix-cache `NONE_HASH`
   deterministic by default and therefore changes block hash values.
3. **Re-measure before comparing.** FlashInfer moves 0.6.15.post1 to 0.6.18 and
   the `transformers` floor 5.5.3 to 5.10.4 (§9). `6a962071bd` vllm#52998 flips
   `VLLM_ALLREDUCE_USE_FLASHINFER` on by default, which moves the vLLM production
   configuration used as the denominator for any multi-GPU parity run.
4. **Build the target and measure `vllm.__version__`** before writing the
   `parity-pin` block. Never transcribe it. The unshallow removes the
   `setuptools_scm` obstacle (#1185), so the `+g<sha>` string is obtainable.
5. **Tier the 356 INVENTORY entries** (C4) and update
   `porting-inventory.md` §9 for the four deviations, in the same cycle.

## 12. The KV cache series, sized against this tree (PINPORT, #2524)

§11 item 2 names the KV cache layout series as the first thing the next wave
takes, and calls `8bdc70ec7b` "the largest single item in the queue at 1797 lines
over 30 ported files". That is the UPSTREAM size. This section is the size on
OUR side, derived by reading the four upstream diffs against the tree at
`63889449c`. It is a static reading; nothing was built.

**The portable content is roughly 15 to 20 percent of upstream's volume:** about
6 core files, 8 model registries, 4 test files and 500 to 800 lines, against
upstream's 1797 over 30. The reduction is not optimism. It is that two of stage
6's three parts land on concepts this tree does not carry.

| Commit | Disposition here | Our size |
|---|---|---|
| `61874f9842` vllm#51612 stage 4 | REAL_GAP | ~80-140 lines, 2 src + 1 test |
| `57bd0ed441` vllm#51704 stage 5 | REAL_GAP, narrower than upstream | 4 core + 2 test files |
| `8bdc70ec7b` vllm#51718 stage 6 | SPLITS three ways, see below | ~150-250 lines |
| `e126687a9a` vllm#53896 | REAL_GAP for the generic parts | ~120-200 lines |

**Stage 6 splits, and only one third of it is portable.** (1) `storage_block_size`
becoming `num_states`/`tokens_per_state`, and deleting `compress_ratio`, is a
REAL_GAP and is the ~150-250 lines above. (2) The explicit `KVCacheLayout` stride
permutation is **NOT_APPLICABLE**: `grep -rE 'KVCacheLayout|LBHNC|LBNHC|VLLM_KV_CACHE_LAYOUT'
src/ include/` returns **0** executable hits, because this tree allocates one
contiguous per-layer buffer and builds a single hard-wired NHD view
(`src/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/runner.cpp:1006-1104`), a choice recorded in prose for
every backend at `src/vllm/v1/attention/backend.cpp:388-443`. (3) The
group-to-tensor planning pipeline is **NOT_APPLICABLE** too: we have no
`get_kv_cache_config_from_groups` and no `UniformTypeKVCacheSpecs`, and
`KVCacheTensor` is declared at `include/vllm/v1/kv_cache_interface.h:489-494` and
never read outside tests. Parts (2) and (3) are campaigns, not diffs. They belong
under `## Owed` with an owning row rather than inside this series.

**Three hazards the next wave should plan for, and the first is the dangerous
one.**

1. **The `head_size_v` hoist in stage 5 is a compiles-clean, wrong-page-size
   failure.** Stage 5 moves `head_size_v` up from the subclasses onto
   `AttentionSpec`. Our spec constructors are POSITIONAL with defaults, and
   `head_size_v`, `sliding_window` and `attention_chunk_size` are all `int`, so
   moving a parameter up the hierarchy silently re-binds existing call sites
   where the types happen to match — across about 8 model registries. A gate that
   only compiles will not see it. Convert those constructors to named-parameter
   structs or designated-initializer aggregates FIRST, as its own commit, before
   any of stages 4 to 6.
2. **Stage 4 cannot be applied as a diff, because this tree is behind the PIN on
   the same function.** `unify_hybrid_kv_cache_specs`
   (`src/vllm/v1/core/kv_cache_utils.cpp:26-93`) lacks the
   `full_attention_block_size` recompute, the `promoted_page_size_padded`
   recompute, the `SlidingWindowMLASpec` branch and the `is_uniform_type` early
   return that upstream ALREADY had at `555967922`. The wave must bring it to pin
   state first, then apply stage 4 on top. That preparatory work is invisible in
   §4 and needs its own sizing. A latent defect follows from the same gap:
   `SlidingWindowMLASpec` derives from `SlidingWindowSpec`
   (`include/vllm/v1/kv_cache_interface.h:397`), so our
   `dynamic_cast<const SlidingWindowSpec*>` matches it and would promote it to a
   plain `FullAttentionSpec`, dropping `cache_dtype_str` and `compress_ratio`.
3. **Three of the four commits' primary targets are UNREACHED here**, so a wave
   that edits only them lands nothing reachable from a production entry point and
   fails §"Nothing lands dead". `unify_hybrid_kv_cache_specs` has no production
   caller (verified: its definition, its declaration, one comment in
   `hybrid_kv_budget.h`, and `tests/vllm/v1/test_kv_cache_utils.cpp`).
   `resolve_kv_cache_block_sizes` has none either, because the scheduler still
   passes `hash_block_size = block_size`
   (`src/vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.cpp:277`) and
   `.agents/specs/prefix-match-unit.md:76` records that threading as owed.
   `KVCacheTensor` is a dead struct. The wave must either wire one of them or
   name what is unreached, its owning row and its issue, in both the commit body
   and the pull request body.

**One UNCERTAIN.** Whether stage 5's `state_content_bytes` is the right home for
our `kFp8DsMlaV4TokenBytes` (584) and `kFp8DsMlaV32TokenBytes` (656) constants,
given we have no `customize_spec` call site and our specs are built by model
registries rather than by an attention layer. Settled by reading how
`vllm/models/deepseek_v4/attention.py` and `vllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/`
publish those bytes at `cdefd9d499` after stage 5, then deciding whether our
registry factory becomes the publisher or we add a backend hook.

## 13. The worked list: the real gaps the re-derivation found (PINPORT, #2524)

These are the 11 entries out of 63 sampled that are genuinely absent from this
tree. They are the porting queue, and each carries its own evidence so the next
wave does not re-derive it. Line references are at `63889449c`.

**Two are LIVE DEFECTS on reachable paths, not missing forward-ports.**

- `4d341ca829` vllm#49206 — priority-scheduler preemption drops the next
  request. Now [#2527](https://github.com/mudler/vllm.cpp/issues/2527), with the
  reconciled patch attached. `src/vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.cpp:739,762`.
- `a0c092ee72` vllm#48245 — async scheduling underflows the placeholder count
  after a preemption. Now
  [#2531](https://github.com/mudler/vllm.cpp/issues/2531).
  `Scheduler::preempt_request`
  (`src/vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.cpp:345-382`) never zeroes
  `num_output_placeholders`, and `src/vllm/v1/core/sched/async_scheduler.cpp:94`
  then decrements unconditionally. **The `assert` on the next line is compiled
  out under `NDEBUG`**, so a release build does not trap: the count goes
  negative silently. Upstream replaced the whole mechanism with
  `num_stale_output_tokens` / `drop_stale_output`.

**Ranked for value, highest first.**

1. `68ca6fd02c` vllm#50869 — **delete** the DSpark refusal at
   `include/vllm/config/speculative.h:364-376`, which throws when
   `num_speculative_tokens < dspark_block_size`. Pure deletion, and a
   user-visible unblock: configurations that start on upstream today hard-fail
   here. **This tree WIDENED the check** beyond upstream's, falling back to
   `block_size` when `dspark_block_size` is absent
   (`src/vllm/entrypoints/model_loader.cpp:905`), so it fires on published Qwen3
   drafts where upstream's original never did. **This resolves ordering
   constraint C9**, see below.
2. `23c0f337b7` vllm#51180 — one-line predicate swap at
   `src/vllm/v1/core/kv_cache_utils.cpp:680`, from
   `m->block_size != cache_block_size` to `mamba_cache_mode != "align"`. The
   field already exists (`include/vllm/v1/kv_cache_interface.h:481`) and is read
   elsewhere.
3. `7303c66f68` vllm#48171 — one of five arms is absent: the nested-unescaped-
   quote recovery, plus the streaming `contains_broken_string_literal`
   withholding. The other four are satisfied by our hand-written parser rather
   than `ast.parse`. Today `command='sed -n '1,9p' f.py'` drops the whole tool
   call (`src/vllm/entrypoints/openai/tool_parsers/lfm2.cpp:104-106`).
4. `0a94d85a66` vllm#51865 — the `has_prefill` conjunct is absent at `q == 1`
   (`include/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/cudagraph_dispatch.h:168` returns early), so a
   batch of 1-token prefill chunks dispatches as uniform decode. For `q > 1` we
   are already stricter than upstream. Needs a prefill signal threaded into the
   runner; only GDN has one today (`src/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/runner.cpp:2635`).

   **CORRECTED 2026-09-03 (PORTQ-4, #2680): the PATH in this entry does not
   exist.** There is no `include/` copy of `cudagraph_dispatch.h`; the file is at
   `src/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/cudagraph_dispatch.h`, and the symbol is
   `GraphEligibleQueryLen` at `:162-176` with the early return at `:168`. The
   LINE NUMBER and the SUBSTANCE of this entry are both correct and are left as
   written; re-verified at `a700e8da6`, and now filed as
   [#2694](https://github.com/mudler/vllm.cpp/issues/2694) against
   `ENG-CUDAGRAPH-BREAK`. This is the SECOND finding of the same wrong path in
   this file: `2026-09-03-portq3.md` §2.4 corrected
   `include/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/cudagraph_dispatch.h:163` for its entry [101] and
   repaired it only inside that report, so the source went on to mislead a fourth
   reader. Correcting the source is what stops the next one. PORTQ-4 also found a
   second conjunct the entry does not name: `pure_decode` at
   `src/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/runner.cpp:2603-2604` has the same hole, and its
   `gdn_meta.num_prefill_tokens == 0` term is inert on every non-GDN model.
5. `e76b71f659` vllm#51179 — list-valued `num_experts_per_tok` in `hf_config`.
   One line, latent.
6. `923949e6e3` vllm#49984 — the `request_num_preemptions` metric is not
   exported. Bounded.
7. `46638857fd` vllm#52830 — `serving_chat` drops the reasoning parser when an
   engine-backed parser is active.
8. `481839ad9e` vllm#53388 — `disable_eagle_block_drop` and
   `use_eagle_block_drop()` beside `use_eagle()`
   (`include/vllm/config/speculative.h:479`). Opt-in, zero default delta, and low
   value while `src/vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.cpp:279` hard-codes
   `use_eagle=false`.
9. `f57123aa2d` vllm#48048 — `session_id` API surface. API-only.

### C9 is resolved, and not by ordering

Carry-over C9 warns that `5789897aa4` vllm#49969 ADDS a DSpark block-size
validation that `68ca6fd02c` and `51def7848a` then REMOVE, so SHA-ordered
porting installs a check and deletes it.

**This tree already has the check**, and in a widened form, so the hazard does
not arise: `5789897aa4` is ALREADY_SATISFIED and must simply be skipped, and only
the removal is ported. Porting the pair in order would have been wasted work
either way; porting only the first would have kept a refusal upstream no longer
has.

### Two features surfaced as owed debt rather than gaps

- `max_num_new_slots_for_drafting` and the whole drafting-slot reservation in
  `max_num_scheduled_tokens` (`2ac1f683f1` vllm#51256) are unported entirely, not
  merely out of date: `include/vllm/config/scheduler.h:165-167` resolves
  `max_num_scheduled_tokens` as `value_or(max_num_batched_tokens)` and never
  subtracts a drafting delta.
- `index_share_for_mtp_iteration` is parsed at
  `src/vllm/model_executor/models/glm_moe_dsa.cpp:371-372` and stored at
  `include/vllm/model_executor/models/glm_moe_dsa.h:132` with **zero** consumers.
  An inert config key.

### Two comments whose premise upstream falsified

Both conclusions still hold, so these are documentation repairs rather than
dispatch defects. They matter because a reader grounding a future decision in
either line would be reading a stale upstream.

- `src/vt/cuda/cuda_matmul.cu:705` states the CUDA fp8 kernel order as
  "Marlin -> FlashInfer -> Cutlass -> PerTensorTorch"; after `bca7bea240`
  vllm#52182 Marlin is next-to-last.
- `include/vt/ops.h:2911-2913` justifies choosing CUTLASS partly with "Marlin is
  excluded at cc >= 89", which is the exclusion that commit deleted.
